2>_{\omega}\ldots>_{\omega}i>_{\omega}1>_{\omega}i+1\ldots>_{\omega}n.

We aim to show that
\mathrm{I}\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
to reach a contradiction. Fix
A\subseteq[n]
and
j\in S
. Suppose
1\in A
, it follows that
M(A,\mathrm{I})=M(A,v^{j})
. Thus, we have that
\bigtriangleup_{A}(\mathrm{I},v^{j})=\emptyset
. This yields,

\bigtriangleup_{A}(\omega,\mathrm{I})\sqcup\bigtriangleup_{A}(\mathrm{I},v^{j})=\bigtriangleup_{A}(\omega,v^{j}).

If
1\notin A
, then
M(A,\mathrm{I})=M(A,\omega)
and hence
\bigtriangleup_{A}(\omega,\mathrm{I})=\emptyset
. This yields,

\bigtriangleup_{A}(\omega,\mathrm{I})\sqcup\bigtriangleup_{A}(\mathrm{I},v^{j})=\bigtriangleup_{A}(\omega,v^{j}).

By definition of
{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}
, we have that

(29)

{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,v^{j}\right)={\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,\mathrm{I}\right)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\mathrm{I},v^{j}\right).

By (
29
) and the triangle inequality we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,v^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in S}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,v^{j}\right)+\sum_{j\in T}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,v^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j\in S}\left[{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,\mathrm{I}\right)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\mathrm{I},v^{j}\right)\right]+\sum_{j\in T}\left[{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\mathrm{I},v^{j}\right)-{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,\mathrm{I}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\left(|S|-|T|\right){\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\omega,\mathrm{I}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\mathrm{I},v^{j}\right)\geq\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\mathrm{I},v^{j}\right),

where the last inequality follows from observing that
|S|\geq|T|
. Since
\omega\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
it follows that
\mathrm{I}\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
, but then we get
1\in W^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
, a contradiction.
∎

Proof of Proposition
18
.

Fix
k\leq n
and
V=(v^{1},\ldots,v^{m})\in{\mathbf{V}_{n}}
with
v^{i}(\left[k\right])=\left[k\right]
for all
i\in\left[m\right]
. Suppose there exists
v\in P_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
such that
v([k])\neq[k]
. We have that
|[k]\cap v((k,n])|=|(k,n]\cap v([k])|\neq 0
. We now consider the transposition
t_{a,b}
of

a:=M([k]\cap v((k,n]),v)\ \ \ \textnormal{and}\ \ \ b:=m((k,n]\cap v([k]),v),

where we observe that, by definition,
a<b
, but
b>_{v}a
. Let
\tilde{v}:=t_{a,b}v
. We prove that for all
i\in[m]
,

\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\tilde{v},v^{i})<\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(v,v^{i}).

Notice that, by possibly relabeling the candidates, we can assume wlog that
v^{i}=\mathrm{I}
. Let now
a^{\prime}\in[k]
be such that
v(a^{\prime})=b
and
b^{\prime}\in(k,n]
be such that
v(b^{\prime})=a
. Clearly, we have
a^{\prime}<b^{\prime}
. To ease the notation we define
j^{\downarrow=}:=j^{\downarrow}\cup\{j\}
, and, similarly,
j^{\uparrow=}:=j^{\uparrow}\cup\{j\}
, for all
j\in[n]
. To better grasp the subsequent steps of the proof the reader can refer to the following illustration.

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccc|ccccccccccc}\mathrm{I}&=&1&\ldots&a-1&a&a+1&\ldots&a^{\prime}-1&a^{\prime}&a^{\prime}+1&\ldots&k&k+1&\ldots&b^{\prime}-1&b^{\prime}&b^{\prime}+1&\ldots&b-1&b&b+1&\ldots&n\\
\tilde{v}&=&v_{1}&\ldots&v_{a-1}&v_{a}&v_{a+1}&\ldots&v_{a^{\prime}-1}&a&v_{a^{\prime}+1}&\ldots&v_{k}&v_{k+1}&\ldots&v_{b^{\prime}-1}&b&v_{b^{\prime}+1}&\ldots&v_{b-1}&v_{b}&v_{b+1}&\ldots&v_{n}\\
v&=&v_{1}&\ldots&v_{a-1}&v_{a}&v_{a+1}&\ldots&v_{a^{\prime}-1}&b&v_{a^{\prime}+1}&\ldots&v_{k}&v_{k+1}&\ldots&v_{b^{\prime}-1}&a&v_{b^{\prime}+1}&\ldots&v_{b-1}&v_{b}&v_{b+1}&\ldots&v_{n}\\
\end{array}

Figure 2.
Here we illustrate the three permutations in the case with
a<a^{\prime}
and
b>b^{\prime}
.

We can see that the following facts hold true.

(i)

If
j\in[1,a)
, then
j^{\downarrow}
contains both
a
and
b
and thus
|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,v}|=|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}|
.

(ii)

If
j\in(b,n]
, then
j^{\downarrow}
contains neither
a
nor
b
and thus
|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,v}|=|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}|
.

(iii)

If
j=a
, then
a^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}=(b^{\downarrow,v}\setminus\{a\})\cup\{b\}
. Notice that
a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}\subseteq a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\lvert(a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}})\setminus(a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v})\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert(a^{\downarrow}\cap((b^{\downarrow,v}\setminus\{a\})\cup\{b\}))\setminus(a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v})\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert(a^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow=,v})\setminus(a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v})\rvert

\displaystyle=|a^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow=,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}|\geq|\{b\}|=1.

(iv)

If
j\in(a,b)
,
j^{\downarrow}
contains
b
but not
a
. Moreover,
j^{\downarrow,v}=j^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}
iff
v^{-1}(j)<a^{\prime}
(i.e.,
j>_{v}b
) or
v^{-1}(j)>b^{\prime}
(i.e.,
a>_{v}j
). Notice that
v^{-1}(j)
cannot be neither
a^{\prime}
nor
b^{\prime}
. On the other hand, if
v^{-1}(j)\in(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
, then
j^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}=\{b\}\cup(j^{\downarrow,v}\setminus\{a\})
, and

|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow\tilde{v}}|=|(j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow v})\sqcup\{b\}|=1+|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow v}|.

(v)

If
j=b
,
b^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}=a^{\downarrow,v}
. Notice that
b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}\subseteq b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}
. Moreover,

\lvert(b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v})\setminus(b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v})\rvert=|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}|.

Notice that
b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}\subsetneq a^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow=,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}
as
b
belongs to the second but not to the first set and
b^{\downarrow}\subseteq a^{\downarrow}
.

Finally, we evaluate the difference between the two distances:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(v,\mathrm{I})-\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\tilde{v},\mathrm{I})}{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,v}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)-\left(f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}|)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\in(a,b)}(f_{\bm{\beta}}(|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,v}|+1)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|j^{\downarrow}\cap j^{\downarrow,v}|))\mathbf{1}_{v^{-1}(j)\in(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}

\displaystyle{\geq}

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,\tilde{v}}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)-\left(f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|+|a^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow=,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)

\displaystyle-\left(f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|+|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{>}

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|+|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)

\displaystyle-\left(f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|+|b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}|)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|)\right)\overset{(b)}{\geq}0,

where (
a
) follows from
b^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}\subsetneq a^{\downarrow}\cap b^{\downarrow=,v}\cap a^{\uparrow=,v}
and Lemma
A.3
while (
b
) holds by combining Lemma
A.4
and
|a^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|\geq|b^{\downarrow}\cap a^{\downarrow,v}|
.

∎

Proof of Corollary
20
.

Let
J=\{j\in[n]:\forall i,i^{\prime}\in[m],v^{i}([j])=v^{i^{\prime}}([j])\}
this is non empty since
n\in J
. Since
P_{\bm{\beta}}
has the blockwise Pareto winner property, for any
j\in J
,
A_{j}:=v^{1}([j])
is such that for any
v\in P_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
,
v([j])=A_{j}
. Moreover, for all
j\in J
and
v\in P_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
,
v((j,n])=A_{j}^{\mathsf{c}}
.

Now, given any sequence
(k_{j})_{j=0}^{\ell}
as in the definition of the partitionwise Pareto property, we have that for all
j\in[\ell]
,
k_{j}\in J
and, by setting
A_{0}=\emptyset
, we have that
v^{i}((k_{j-1},k_{j}])=A_{k_{j}}\setminus A_{k_{j-1}}
for all
i\in[m]
. But then,
for any
v\in P_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
, we have that

v((k_{j-1},k_{j}])=v((0,k_{j}]\setminus(0,k_{j-1}])=v((0,k_{j}])\setminus v((0,k_{j-1}])=A_{k_{j}}\setminus A_{k_{j-1}}.

∎

Proof of Proposition

, for all

.

21

Notice that this implies that while

, for all

V=(5(1,2,3,4,\ldots,n),4(3,2,1,4,\ldots,n),1(2,3,1,4,\ldots,n)).

\underset{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}}{\min}\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\sum_{A\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\beta_{|A|}\mu(M(A,\sigma)\triangle M(A,v^{j}))\geq\sum_{A\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\beta_{|A|}\min\limits_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mu(M(A,\sigma)\triangle M(A,v^{j})).

and

By using

, for

, clearly no
x\geq 4
(if any) can be optimal. Observe

(32)

\displaystyle M(A,\sigma)=1\implies\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mu\left(M(A,\sigma)\triangle M(C,v)\right)=5\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+4\mu_{3}

\displaystyle M(A,\sigma)=2\implies\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mu\left(M(A,\sigma)\triangle M(A,v)\right)=5\mu_{1}+9\mu_{2}+4\mu_{3}

\displaystyle M(A,\sigma)=3\implies\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mu\left(M(A,\sigma)\triangle M(A,v)\right)=5\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+6\mu_{3}.

Recall that, under our assumption that
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\leq\cdots\leq\mu_{n}
and

, for

, we have that, for

,
\mu_{i}>0
; in particular,
\mu_{2}>0
and
\mu_{3}>0
. Therefore, any permutation such that
M(A,\sigma)=1
is optimal. Clearly,
\mathrm{I}
is optimal. By using Inequality (
31
), we conclude that
\mathrm{I}\in P^{\mu}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(V)
.

Finally, suppose that
\beta_{k}>0
for some
k\geq 3
and let
\pi\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
be any permutation such that

. Let
A
be a set size

that contains

,

\pi

, we have that
\pi\notin P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
.

Using the same profile of voters (
30
) we can show that
(i)
implies
(iii)
. We have that
1,2\in\mathcal{C}(V)
. Indeed,
n_{1,j}(V)\geq 0
and
n_{2,j}(V)\geq 0
for all
j\in[n]
. Though, following exactly the same steps as in the previous implication we have that unless
{\beta}_{k}=0
, for all
k\geq 3
,
1\in W^{\mu}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(V)
while
2\notin W^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
.

Now we prove that
(iii)
implies both
(i)
and
(ii)
.
Fix
c,d\in[n]
and
\sigma,\pi\in\mathop{}\!\mathbb{S}_{n}
such that they differ only on comparing
c,d
, without loss of generality say
d>_{\sigma}c
and
c>_{\pi}d
. Notice that for all
V\in\mathbf{V}_{n}
, given that
\beta_{k}=0
for all
k\geq 3
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\sigma,V\right)-{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\pi,V\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\sigma,v^{j}\right)-{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\pi,v^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mu\left(\bigtriangleup_{\left\{c,d\right\}}(\sigma,v^{j})\right)-\mu\left(\bigtriangleup_{\left\{c,d\right\}}(\pi,v^{j})\right)

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}\left[\sum_{j:c>_{v^{j}}d}\mu\left(\bigtriangleup_{\left\{c,d\right\}}(\sigma,v^{j})\right)-\sum_{j:d>_{v^{j}}c}\mu\left(\bigtriangleup_{\left\{c,d\right\}}(\pi,v^{j})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}\left[\sum_{j:c>_{v^{j}}d}\mu\left(\left\{c,d\right\}\right)-\sum_{j:d>_{v^{j}}c}\mu\left(\left\{c,d\right\}\right)\right]

(33)

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}n_{c,d}(V)\mu\left(\left\{c,d\right\}\right).

First we show that
(iii)
implies
(i)
. Suppose that
W_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\mu}
does not satisfy the Condorcet principle. Then, there exists
V\in{\mathbf{V}_{n}}
and
c\in\mathcal{C}(V)
such that for all
\sigma\in P^{\mu}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(V)
,
c\notin M(C,\sigma)
. Therefore, for all
\sigma\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
there exists
d\in C
such that
(d,c)\in\textnormal{adj}(\sigma)
. Let
\pi:=t_{d,c}\sigma
. Since
c\in\mathcal{C}(V)
, the previous equalities yield that

{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\sigma,V\right)-{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\pi,V\right)=\beta_{2}n_{c,d}(V)\mu\left(\left\{c,d\right\}\right)\geq 0,

and hence,
\pi\in P^{\mu}_{{\bm{\beta}}}
. Repeating the same steps finitely many times, we have that there must exist some
\omega\in P^{\mu}_{{\bm{\beta}}}
such that
c\in M(C,\omega)
, a contradiction. Therefore,
W^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}
satisfies the Condorcet principle.

Now we show that
(iii)
implies
(ii)
. For the sake of contradiction, suppose that
P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}
does not satisfy the Condorcet principle. Then, only two cases can happen.

(1)

There exist
c,d\in C
and
V\in\mathbf{V}_{n}
such that
n_{c,d}(V)>0
and some
\sigma\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
with
(d,c)\in\mathrm{adj}(\sigma)
. By (
33
), since
\beta_{2}>0
and
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{++}_{n}
,
{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(t_{d,c}\sigma,V)<{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,V)
contradicting the minimality of
\sigma
.

(2)

There exist
c,d\in C
and
V\in\mathbf{V}_{n}
such that
n_{c,d}(V)=0
, there is some
\sigma\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
such that
(c,d)\in\mathrm{adj}(\sigma)
, but there is no
\pi\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
such that
(d,c)\in\mathrm{adj}(\pi)
.
Though, by (
33
),
\pi:=t_{c,d}\sigma
satisfies
{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\sigma,V\right)={\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}\left(\pi,V\right)
and hence
\pi\in P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
, a contradiction.

Thus,
P^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}
satisfies the Condorcet principle.
∎

F.
Proofs in §
6

Proof of Theorem
23
.

Let
\sigma,\pi\in\mathop{}\!\mathbb{S}_{n}
. Because of neutrality we can assume, without loss of generality, that
\pi=\mathrm{I}
. We conveniently define
\lVert\sigma\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}:=\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\mathrm{I})/2
and
\lVert\sigma\rVert^{\textnormal{apx}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}:=\mathrm{d}^{\textnormal{apx}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\mathrm{I})/2
. We start showing that
\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}(\sigma,\pi)\leq\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi)
. First of all, notice that

\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}(\sigma,\pi)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma}|)+f_{\bm{\beta}}(|{j}^{\downarrow,\pi}|)-2\min\left(f_{\bm{\beta}}(|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma}|),f_{\bm{\beta}}(|{j}^{\downarrow,\pi}|)\right).

Therefore, since
|A\cap B|\leq\min(|A|,|B|)
and
f_{\bm{\beta}}
is increasing (Lemma
A.3
), it holds that

\displaystyle\lVert\sigma\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}-\lVert\sigma\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}(\min(|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma}|),n-j)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma}\cap\ \{{j+1},\ldots,{n}\}|)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}(\min(|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma}|),n-j)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(\min(|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma}|,n-j))\right]=0,

and this implies that
\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi)\geq\mathrm{d}^{\textnormal{apx}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi).

Now we show that
\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi)\leq\gamma_{{\bm{\beta}}}\mathrm{d}_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}(\sigma,\pi)
. If
\sigma=\pi
the claim is trivial. Therefore suppose that
\sigma\neq\mathrm{I}
. Denote
\sigma^{0}:=\sigma
, and consider the following procedure to transform
\sigma^{0}
into the identity
\mathrm{I}
.

Inputs:

Permutation
\sigma^{0}

Set
k=0

for

i\in\{n,n-1,\ldots,1\}

do

while

\sigma^{k}_{i}\neq{i}

do

\sigma^{k+1}\leftarrow\sigma^{k}

{\sigma^{k+1}_{\sigma^{k}_{i}}}\leftarrow{\sigma^{k}_{i}}
and

{\sigma^{k+1}_{i}}\leftarrow{\sigma^{k}_{\sigma^{k}_{i}}}

k\leftarrow k+1

end while

end for

Algorithm 2

Bottom-focus sorting

Notice that Algorithm
2
reaches the identity in
O(n^{2})
steps. Denote by
K
the number of transformations necessary to convert
\sigma^{0}
into
\mathrm{I}
. Now, consider the transition from
\sigma^{k}
to
\sigma^{k+1}
. Let
s
be the greatest integer in
[n]
such that
\sigma^{k}_{s}\neq s
. We have

\displaystyle\sigma^{k}

\displaystyle=\left({\sigma^{k}_{1}},\cdots,{\sigma^{k}_{r-1}},{\sigma^{k}_{r}},\cdots,{\sigma^{k}_{s}},{\sigma^{k}_{s+1}},\cdots,{\sigma^{k}_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\sigma^{k+1}

\displaystyle=\left({\sigma^{k}_{1}},\cdots,{\sigma^{k}_{r-1}},{\sigma^{k}_{s}},\cdots,{\sigma^{k}_{r}},{\sigma^{k}_{s+1}},\cdots,{\sigma^{k}_{n}}\right)

As, by design, the procedure works from the bottom to the top, and, by assumption, we are up-ranking
{\sigma^{k}_{s}}
, necessarily all alternatives in positions from
s+1
to
n
have already been matched. That is
{\sigma^{k}_{i}}=i
, for all
i\geq s+1
.
23
23

23

We understand that when
s=n
, no element has been previously matched.
As all elements from position
s+1
to
n
have been matched, it holds that, for all
i\leq s
,
\sigma^{k}_{i}\leq s
, and, in particular,
\sigma^{k}_{r}\leq s
. Furthermore, as we are swapping
{\sigma^{k}_{s}}
with
{\sigma^{k}_{r}}
, we have
r=\sigma^{k}_{s}
and
s>\sigma^{k}_{s}
. Finally, notice that
\sigma^{k}_{r}\neq r=\sigma^{k}_{s}
.

Let
\Delta_{k}:=\lVert\sigma^{k}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}-\lVert\sigma^{k+1}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}
and
\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}:=\lVert\sigma^{k}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}-\lVert\sigma^{k+1}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}
. Then, we have
\lVert\sigma^{0}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Delta_{k}
and
\lVert\sigma^{0}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}
, as
\lVert\mathrm{I}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\textnormal{apx}}=\lVert\mathrm{I}\rVert_{{\bm{\beta}}}=0
. The following holds

(34)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-j,|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}|\right)\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-j,|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}|\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\sum_{j=r,\sigma^{k}_{r}}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-j,|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}|\right)\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-j,|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}|\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-r,n-r\right)\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-r,n-s\right)\right)

\displaystyle+f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-\sigma^{k}_{r},n-s\right)\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\min\left(n-\sigma^{k}_{r},n-r\right)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-r\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-\max\left(r,\sigma^{k}_{r}\right)\right)\geq 0,

where (
a
) holds because
\sigma^{k}
and
\sigma^{k+1}
disagree only on candidates
r
and
{\sigma^{k}_{r}}
, and (
b
) holds because, by construction,
s>r
and, as argued above,
\sigma^{k}_{r}\leq s
. Further, note that, by applying Lemma
A.3
, we conclude that
\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}>0
if and only if
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
.
For the non-approximated version the analysis is more delicate. We have

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}\cap\{{j+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{j}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}\cap\{{j+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\sum_{j=r}^{s}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{j}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{j}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)\right],

where (
a
) holds because only the alternatives in positions from
r
to
s
are affected by the transformation.
We handle the cases separately.

Case
j=r
.

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{r}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{r}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\bm{\beta}}(|\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}|)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cup\{{\sigma^{k}_{s}}\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\cap\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-s)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}},{\sigma^{k}_{s}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-s)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}},{r}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-s)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|+\mathbf{1}(r\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\})\right)

where (
a
) holds because
\sigma^{k}_{s}=r
.

Case
j=s
.

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{s}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{s}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{s}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{s}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cup\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}}\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\cap\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{s}+1},\ldots,s\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}|)

\displaystyle=f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}},{\sigma^{k}_{r}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{s}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-s)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{r+1},\ldots,s\}\right|+\mathbf{1}({\sigma^{k}_{r}}\in\{{r+1},\ldots,s\})\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-s),

where (
a
) holds because
\sigma^{k}_{s}=r
.

Cases
r<j<s
.

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{j}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k+1}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma^{k}_{j}}^{\downarrow,\sigma^{k}}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cup\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}}\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\cap\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cup\{{\sigma^{k}_{s}}\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\cap\left(\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}\cup\{{s+1},\ldots,{n}\}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}},{\sigma^{k}_{r}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}},{\sigma^{k}_{s}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|+\mathbf{1}({\sigma^{k}_{r}}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\})\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|+\mathbf{1}({r}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\})\right),

where (
a
) holds because
\sigma^{k}_{s}=r.

Taking into account all cases, we have established that

(35)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{k}^{r}+\sum_{j=r+1}^{s-1}\Delta_{k}^{j},

where, to ease notation, we define for all
r<j<s

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}^{j}

\displaystyle:=f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}|+\mathbf{1}({\sigma^{k}_{r}}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\})\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}|+\mathbf{1}({r}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\})\right)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}^{r}

\displaystyle:=f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{r+1},\ldots,s\}|+\mathbf{1}({\sigma^{k}_{r}}\in\{{r+1},\ldots,s\})\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}|+\mathbf{1}({r}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\})\right).

Claim
2
.

If
\sigma^{k}_{r}<r
then, for all
j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\cup\{r\}
,
\Delta_{k}^{j}\leq 0
, and
\Delta_{k}\leq 0.

Proof.

First of all, observe that, for all
j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}
, if
{\sigma^{k}_{r}}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}
, then also
{r}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}
. Therefore,
\Delta_{k}^{j}=0
. Further, as
\sigma^{k}_{r}<r
, we have that
\mathbf{1}({\sigma^{k}_{r}}\in\{{r+1},\ldots,s\})=0
, and
\mathbf{1}({r}\in\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\})=1
. Therefore, it holds

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}^{r}

\displaystyle\leq f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|\right)

\displaystyle-f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(n-s+\left|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}\right|+1\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}0,

where (
a
) holds because
f_{\bm{\beta}}
is non-decreasing (Lemma
A.3
)
∎

By using Claim
2
, we have established that, whenever
\sigma^{k}_{r}<r
,
\Delta_{k}\leq 0
.

In the following, we discuss the case
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
. The same arguments underlying Claim
2
yield

Claim
3
.

If
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
then, for all
j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\cup\{r\}
,
\Delta_{k}^{j}\geq 0
, and
\Delta_{k}\geq 0.

Furthermore, if
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
, for all
j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}
, it holds that

\neg\left(r<\sigma^{k}_{j}+1\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\Delta_{k}^{j}=0.

Further, by recalling that
\sigma^{k}_{s}=r
(and so
\sigma^{k}_{j}\neq r
), we have

r<\sigma^{k}_{j}+1\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}\iff r+1\leq\sigma^{k}_{j}\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1.

Let
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{k}}:=\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\mid r+1\leq\sigma^{k}_{j}\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}
. To ease the notation we write
\mathcal{J}_{k}:=\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{k}}
. We have shown that

(36)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{k}^{r}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}}\Delta_{k}^{j},\;\textnormal{with}\;\Delta_{k}^{j}\geq 0,\;\textnormal{for all }j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}\cup\{r\}.

Case 1.

Suppose
r<\sigma^{k}_{r}<s
. In such case we consider the permutation
\omega
defined as follows

(37)

\displaystyle\omega_{r}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{k}_{r}\;\textnormal{and}\;\omega_{s}=\sigma^{k}_{s}=r

\displaystyle\omega_{\sigma^{k}_{r}}

\displaystyle=s

\displaystyle\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=i,\;\textnormal{otherwise.}

Case 2.

Suppose
r<\sigma^{k}_{r}=s
. In such case we consider the permutation
\omega
defined as follows

(38)

\displaystyle\omega_{r}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{k}_{r}=s\;\textnormal{and}\;\omega_{s}=\sigma^{k}_{s}=r

\displaystyle\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=i,\;\textnormal{otherwise.}

Observe that the
only
conditions we used to establish (
36
) are:

•

r<s

•

\sigma^{k}_{i}=i
, for all
i\geq s+1

•

\sigma^{k}_{s}=r

•

\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
,

and that for the rest
\sigma^{k}
was arbitrary. In particular, notice that, regardless of whether we are under
Case

1
or
Case

2
, we have that
\omega_{i}=i
, for all
i\geq s+1
,
\omega_{s}=r
and
\omega_{r}=\sigma^{k}_{r}
. Therefore, under the assumption
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
, regardless of whether we are in
Case

1
or
Case

2
, the same steps used to show (
36
) apply and yield

\Delta_{\omega}:=\|\omega\|_{{\bm{\beta}}}-\|\omega t_{r,s}\|_{{\bm{\beta}}}=\Delta^{r}_{\omega}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega}}\Delta^{j}_{\omega},

with
\Delta^{j}_{\omega}\geq 0
, for all
j\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\cup\{r\},
where
\mathcal{J}_{\omega}:=\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\mid r+1\leq\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{r}-1\}
.

Claim
4
.

Under both
Case

1
and
Case

2
,

\mathcal{J}_{\omega}=\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}\;\textnormal{and}\;|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}|\geq|\mathcal{J}_{k}|.

Proof.

First of all, under
Case

2
, it holds

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}|

\displaystyle=|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\mid r+1\leq\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{r}-1\}|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\mid r+1\leq j\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}\mid r+1\leq j\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|=\sigma^{k}_{r}-r-1\overset{(c)}{\geq}|\mathcal{J}_{k}|,

where (
a
) holds because
\omega_{r}=\sigma^{k}_{r}
, and, for all
j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}
,
\omega_{j}=j
; (
b
) holds because
\sigma^{k}_{r}=s
; (
c
) holds because, for any
\tau\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
such that
\tau_{r}=\sigma^{k}_{r}
,

\mathcal{J}_{\tau}:=\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\mid r+1\leq\delta_{j}\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}
has cardinality at most
\sigma^{k}_{r}-r-1
.

Under
Case

1
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}|

\displaystyle=|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\mid r+1\leq\omega_{j}\leq\omega_{r}-1\}|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\setminus\{\sigma^{k}_{r}\}\mid r+1\leq j\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|+\mathbf{1}_{r+1\leq\omega_{\sigma^{k}_{r}}\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\setminus\{\sigma^{k}_{r}\}\mid r+1\leq j\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|+\mathbf{1}_{r+1\leq s\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\setminus\{\sigma^{k}_{r}\}\mid r+1\leq j\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}|\{j\in\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}\mid r+1\leq j\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|=\sigma^{k}_{r}-r-1\geq|\mathcal{J}_{k}|,

where (
a
) holds because
\omega_{r}=\sigma^{k}_{r}
, and, for
j\in\{r+1,\ldots,s-1\}\setminus\{\sigma^{k}_{r}\}
,
\omega_{j}=j
; (
b
) holds because
\omega_{\sigma^{k}_{r}}=s
; (
c
) holds because
\sigma^{k}_{r}<s
; (

) holds because if
j\in[r+1,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1]
, then
j\in[r+1,s-1]\setminus\{\sigma^{k}_{r}\}
, as
r<\sigma^{k}_{r}<s
.
∎

Claim
5
.

Under both
Case
1 and
Case
2,
\Delta_{k}\leq\Delta_{\omega}
.

Proof.

First of all, recall that we are assuming
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
.
We first deal with
\Delta^{r}_{k}
. On the one hand, notice that under both
Case

1
and
Case

2
, we have
\Delta^{r}_{\omega}=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-r)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})
. On the other hand, we have

(39)

\displaystyle|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,s\}|

\displaystyle\leq\min\{|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}|,|\{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1,\ldots,s\}|\}

\displaystyle\leq s-\sigma^{k}_{r},

and

(40)

\displaystyle|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}\}|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|

\displaystyle\leq\min\{|\{{\sigma^{k}_{r+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}|,|\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1\}|\}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{k}_{r}-r-1,

where (
a
) holds because
\sigma^{k}_{r}\notin\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}
. Together these inequalities yield

(41)

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{k}

\displaystyle=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{r+1,\ldots,s\}|+1)

\displaystyle-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1,\ldots,s\}|)

\displaystyle=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1,\ldots,s\}|+|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}\}|+1)

\displaystyle-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1,\ldots,s\}|)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+(s-\sigma^{k}_{r})+|\{\sigma^{k}_{r+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{r+1,\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{r}\}|+1)

\displaystyle-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+(s-\sigma^{k}_{r}))

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r}+(\sigma^{k}_{r}-r-1)+1)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})

\displaystyle=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-r)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})=\Delta^{r}_{\omega},

where (
a
) holds by applying Lemma
A.4
and Inequality (
39
), and (
b
) holds by applying Lemma
A.3
and Inequality (
40
). Thus, under both
Case

1
and
Case

2
,
\Delta_{k}^{r}\leq\Delta_{\omega}^{r}
. If
\mathcal{J}_{k}=\emptyset
, then since
\Delta^{j}_{\omega}\geq 0
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
, inequality (
41
) yields
\Delta_{k}=\Delta^{r}_{k}\leq\Delta^{r}_{\omega}\leq\Delta_{\omega}
.

Therefore, in the remaining part of the argument, we assume that
\mathcal{J}_{k}\neq\emptyset
. For all
j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}
, we have

(42)

\displaystyle|\{\sigma^{k}_{j+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}\cap\{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1,\ldots,s\}|

\displaystyle\leq\min(|\{\sigma^{k}_{j+1},\ldots,\sigma^{k}_{s-1}\}|,|\{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1,\ldots,s\}|)

\displaystyle=\min(s-(j+1),s-\sigma^{k}_{j})\leq s-j-1.

We now show that under both
Case

1
and
Case

2
,

(43)

\forall j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}_{\omega},\Delta^{j}_{k}\leq\Delta^{j}_{\omega}.

If
\mathcal{J}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}_{\omega}=\emptyset
, then there is nothing to show, so suppose the intersection is non-empty. Let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
. On the one hand, notice that, under both
Case

1
and
Case

2
, we have
\Delta^{j}_{\omega}=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-j)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-(j+1))
. Therefore, we obtain

(44)

\displaystyle\Delta^{j}_{k}

\displaystyle=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}|+1)

\displaystyle-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+|\{{\sigma^{k}_{j+1}},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{s-1}}\}\cap\{{\sigma^{k}_{j}+1},\ldots,s\}|)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+(s-j-1)+1)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-s+(s-j-1))

\displaystyle=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-j)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-j-1)=\Delta^{j}_{\omega},

where (
a
) holds by Lemma
A.4
and Inequality (
42
).

By combining (
43
) and Inequality (
41
), we obtain that for both
Case

1
and
Case

2
, if
\mathcal{J}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
then
\Delta_{k}\leq\Delta_{\omega}
.

We conclude the analysis of
Case

2
, by showing that
\mathcal{J}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
. Indeed, suppose that
j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}
, but
\omega_{j}\notin\left[r+1,\omega_{r}-1\right]
. If
\omega_{j}<r+1
, then we have two possibilities: either
\omega_{j}=r
which implies
j=s
, or
\omega_{j}<r
, which implies
\omega_{j}=j<r+1
. In both cases we have a contradiction, as
j\in[r+1,s-1]
. If
\omega_{j}>\omega_{r}-1
, then
\omega_{j}>\sigma^{k}_{r}-1
and we have two possibilities: either
\omega_{j}=\sigma^{k}_{r}
which implies
j=r
, or
\omega_{j}>\sigma^{k}_{r}=s
which implies
j=\omega_{j}>s
. In both cases we obtain a contradiction. Thus, under

Case

2
, it holds
\mathcal{J}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
.

Under
Case

1
, it might happen that
\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\neq\emptyset
. Suppose
\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\neq\emptyset
. Since by Claim
4
we have that
|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}|\geq|\mathcal{J}_{k}|
, there exists an injective map
\rho:\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\to\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{k}
. Indeed, notice that

|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{k}|=|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\setminus(\mathcal{J}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}_{\omega})|\overset{(a)}{\geq}|\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus(\mathcal{J}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}_{\omega})|=|\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}|\overset{(b)}{>}0,

where (
a
) holds because
|\mathcal{J}_{\omega}|\geq|\mathcal{J}_{k}|
and we are subtracting from both the same set
\mathcal{J}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
; (
b
) holds under our assumption
\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\neq\emptyset
.
Furthermore, recall that by Claim
4
, we have
\mathcal{J}_{\omega}=[r+1,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1]
, which, in turn, implies that
\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{k}\subseteq[r+1,\sigma^{k}_{r}-1]
.

As
\mathcal{J}_{k}\subseteq[r+1,s-1]
and
\sigma^{k}_{r}<s
, if
j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
necessarily
j\geq\sigma^{k}_{r}>\rho(j)
. Let
j\in\mathcal{J}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
and
z:=\rho(j)
. We show that
\Delta^{j}_{k}\leq\Delta^{z}_{\omega}
. Notice that
\Delta^{z}_{\omega}=f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-z)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-(z+1))
. On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle|\{\omega_{z+1},\ldots,\omega_{s-1}\}\cap\{\omega_{z}+1,\ldots,s\}|

\displaystyle=|\{{z+1},\ldots,{\sigma^{k}_{r}-1},s,{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,{s-1}\}\cap\{{z+1},\ldots,s\}|

\displaystyle=s-z-1>s-j-1,

where we understand that if
z=\sigma^{k}_{r}-1
then

|\{\omega_{z+1},\ldots,\omega_{s-1}\}\cap\{\omega_{z}+1,\ldots,s\}|=|\{s,{\sigma^{k}_{r}+1},\ldots,{s-1}\}\cap\{\sigma^{k}_{r},\ldots,s\}|=s-\sigma^{k}_{r}.

On the other hand, we obtain

(45)

\Delta^{j}_{k}\overset{(a)}{\leq}f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-j)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-j-1)\overset{(b)}{\leq}f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-z)-f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-z-1)=\Delta^{z}_{\omega},

where (
a
) holds by using Inequality (
42
), and (
b
) holds by applying Lemma
A.4
and the fact that
z<j
. By combining (
41
), (
43
), and (
45
), we conclude that, also under
Case

1
,
\Delta_{k}\leq\Delta_{\omega}
.
∎

We can now proceed to compute an upper bound. The following hold

(46)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\Delta_{\omega}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\omega}^{r}+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega}}\Delta_{\omega}^{j}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})+\sum_{j=r+1}^{\sigma^{k}_{r}-1}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-j)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-j-1)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r-1)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})+\sum_{j=r}^{\sigma^{k}_{r}-1}\left[f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-j)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-j-1)\right]

\displaystyle=f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})+f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r-1)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r}),

where (
a
) holds by applying Claim (
5
); (
b
) holds by applying Claim (
4
), and (
c
) holds by adding and subtracting
f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r-1)
. Since
\sigma^{k}_{r}>r
, we have that
\Delta^{\textnormal{apx}}_{k}>0
and

(47)

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{k}}{\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})+f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-(r+1))-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})}{\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})+f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-(r+1))-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})}{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-(r+1))-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})}{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-\sigma^{k}_{r})}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}1+\frac{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-(r+1))}{f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-r)}\leq\gamma_{\bm{\beta}},

where (
a
) holds by using (
46
), and (
b
) follows observing that
x\mapsto\frac{a-x}{b-x}
is decreasing in
[0,b)
for all
a<b
. Finally, we conclude the proof considering all the cases analyzed above. In particular, we define

\mathcal{K}:=\left\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}\mid\sigma^{k+1}=\sigma^{k}t_{r,s},\;\textnormal{for some indices}\;s>r\;\textnormal{and }\sigma^{k}_{r}>r\right\}.

When
k\in\mathcal{K}
, by Claim
3
, we have that
\Delta_{k}\geq 0
and
\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}>0
. If
k\notin\mathcal{K}
, then by Claim
2
we have that
\Delta_{k}\leq 0
and
\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}=0
. Moreover notice that
\mathcal{K}\neq\emptyset
, otherwise, by invoking Claim
2
, we would obtain that
\Delta_{k}\leq 0
, for all
0\leq k\leq K-1
. This, in turn, would imply that
\lVert\sigma^{0}\rVert_{\beta}=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Delta_{k}=0
, which is absurd given that
\sigma^{0}\neq\mathrm{I}
and
\beta_{2}>0.
Thus,
\mathcal{K}\neq\emptyset
. Furthermore, notice that since
\beta_{2}>0
and
\sigma^{0}\neq\mathrm{I}
, we have
\lVert\sigma^{0}\rVert_{\beta}^{\textnormal{apx}}>0.
These observations yield

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\sigma^{0}\rVert_{\beta}}{\lVert\sigma^{0}\rVert_{\beta}^{\textnormal{apx}}}=\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Delta_{k}}{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Delta_{k}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\Delta_{k}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{K}}\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}}\leq\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}.

where (
a
) holds because, when
k\in\mathcal{K}^{c}
,
\Delta_{k}^{\textnormal{apx}}=0
; (
b
) holds because, when
k\in\mathcal{K}^{c}
,
\Delta_{k}\leq 0
, and (
c
) holds by Inequality (
47
).
∎

Proof of Corollary
25
.

Let
\sigma^{\textnormal{apx}}\in P^{\textnormal{apx}}_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
and
\sigma^{*}\in P_{\bm{\beta}}(V)
. The following inequalities hold

\displaystyle\min\limits_{\sigma\in\mathop{}\!\mathbb{S}_{n}}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\sigma,V)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\sigma^{\textnormal{apx}},v^{j})\overset{(a)}{\leq}\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}^{\textnormal{apx}}(\sigma^{\textnormal{apx}},v^{j})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}^{\textnormal{apx}}(\sigma^{*},v^{j})\overset{(c)}{\leq}\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\sigma^{*},v^{j})=\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\min\limits_{\sigma\in\mathop{}\!\mathbb{S}_{n}}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\sigma,V),

where (
a
) and (
c
) hold by using Theorem
23
, and (
b
) holds as, by construction,
\sigma^{\textnormal{apx}}
is a minimizer of
\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}^{\textnormal{apx}}(\cdot,v^{j})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
26
.

Let
(v^{j})_{j=1}^{m}\in{\mathbf{V}_{n}}
.
Construct the following bipartite graph
G=([n],[n],E)
, where each vertex
c\in[n]
corresponds to an alternative in
[n]
, and each vertex
p\in[n]
corresponds to a possible ranking. Edges
e=\{c,p\}\in E
are interpreted as “alternative
c
is in position
p
”. We weight each edge
e=\{c,p\}\in E
by

w_{c,p}=\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\big|f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-p)-f_{\bm{\beta}}(|c^{\downarrow,v^{j}}|)\big|

Observe that we can compute all weights
\{w_{c,p}\}_{c,p}
in time
O(mn^{3})
. Indeed, by using Pascal’s formula, we can pre-compute
\{f_{\bm{\beta}}(0),\ldots,f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-1)\}
in time
O(n^{2})
. Clearly, the number of edges
\{c,p\}
is
n^{2}
. Fix
\{c,p\}
and recall that we have a profile of
m
preferences. For each preference, we need to compute
|c^{\downarrow,v^{j}}|
and this can be done in linear time. Therefore, in total, it takes
O(mn^{3})
to compute all weights.

Further, note that each perfect matching is associated with a linear order
\sigma
, such that
|c^{\downarrow,\sigma}|=n-p
, whenever the edge
\{c,p\}
belongs to the matching. Particularly, by solving for a minimum weight perfect matching, we obtain
\sigma^{\textnormal{apx}}\in\mathop{}\!\mathbb{S}_{n}
that minimizes
\omega\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{|V|}\mathrm{d}^{\textnormal{apx}}_{\bm{\beta}}(\omega,v^{j})
. Finally, we can compute a minimum weight perfect matching by relying on the Hungarian algorithm whose running time is
O(n^{3})
. Thus, the overall running time is
O(mn^{3})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
27
.

Combine Theorem
23
with Corollary
25
and Lemma
26
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
28
.

For all
\sigma,\pi\in\mathop{}\!\mathbb{S}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\textnormal{apx}}_{\mu,{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi)\overset{(a)}{\leq}{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}U\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\beta}}(\sigma,\pi)

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}U\mathrm{d}^{\textnormal{apx}}_{\bm{\beta}}(\sigma,\pi)

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\gamma_{\bm{\beta}}\frac{U}{u}\mathrm{d}^{\textnormal{apx}}_{\mu,{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma,\pi),

where (
a
) holds by applying the same arguments underlying Theorem
23
; (
b
) holds because
\mu
is bounded above, (
c
) holds by applying Theorem
23
, and (
d
) because
\mu
is bounded below.
∎

Proof of Theorem
29
.

Fix
\epsilon>0
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
V=(v^{j})_{j=1}^{m}\in{\mathbf{V}_{n}}
,
{\bm{\beta}}_{2:n}
and
\mu^{(n)}
satisfying the assumption of Theorem
29
. To ease notation, we denote
{\bm{\beta}}={\bm{\beta}}_{2:n}
and
\mu=\mu^{(n)}
. Suppose there exist
q\in\{0,\ldots,n-2\}
alternatives
({\sigma_{1}},\ldots,{\sigma_{q}})
such that, for all
k\leq q
,

\left|\left\{j\in[m]\mid\forall a\in C\setminus\{{\sigma_{1}},\ldots,{\sigma_{k}}\},\ {\sigma_{k}}>_{v^{j}}a\right\}\right|>\frac{m}{2}.

Then, by invoking Proposition
17
, Lemma
A.7
and Remark
A.8
repeatedly, we conclude that there exists an optimal ranking
\sigma^{*}
that assigns alternatives
({\sigma_{1}},\ldots,{\sigma_{q}})
to the first
q
positions. Denote by
\bar{\sigma}
the ranking produced by Algorithm
1
. Observe that, by construction,

(48)

{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;1,q)={\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;1,q)\;\textnormal{and}\;{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+1,q+K)\geq{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+1,q+K).

Suppose that
{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)=0
. As
\beta_{2}>0
, this can happen if and only if
v^{j}=\sigma^{*}
, for all
j\in[m]
. The while-loop in Algorithm
1
ensures that, by construction,
\bar{\sigma}=\sigma^{*}
. Therefore, in the following, we can assume
{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)>0
. It holds

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;1,q)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+1,q+K)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+K+1,n)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;1,q)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+1,q+K)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+K+1,n)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;1,q+K)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+K+1,n)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)-{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+K+1,n)+{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+K+1,n)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{|{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+K+1,n)|}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}+\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V;q+K+1,n)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}1+\frac{|{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+K+1,n)|}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}+\frac{2mU\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}1+\frac{|{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+K+1,n)|}{\frac{um}{2}\phi_{q+1}}+\frac{2mU\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)}{\frac{um}{2}\phi_{q+1}}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}1+\frac{4mU\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)}{\frac{um}{2}\phi_{q+1}}+\frac{2mU\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)}{\frac{um}{2}\phi_{q+1}}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{12U\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)}{u\phi_{q+1}}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{12U\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n-i+1}\beta_{j}\binom{n-i}{j-1}}{u\phi_{q+1}}=1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n}\beta_{j}\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n-j+1}\binom{n-i}{j-1}}{u\phi_{q+1}}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n}\beta_{j}\sum_{r=j-1}^{n-K-q-1}\binom{r}{j-1}}{u\phi_{q+1}}=1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n}\beta_{j}\sum_{r=0}^{n-K-q-j}\binom{j-1+r}{j-1}}{u\phi_{q+1}}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n}\beta_{j}\binom{n-K-q}{n-K-q-j}}{u\phi_{q+1}}=1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n}\beta_{j}\binom{n-K-q}{n-K-q-j}}{u\sum_{j=2}^{n}\beta_{j}\binom{n-(q+1)-1}{j-2}},

\displaystyle=1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n-(q+K)}\beta_{j}\binom{n-(q+K)}{j}}{u\sum_{j=0}^{n-(q+2)}\beta_{j+2}\binom{n-(q+2)}{j}}.

where (
a
) holds by using the relations in (
48
); (
b
) holds by applying Lemma
A.5
; (
c
) holds by applying Lemma
A.6
, and (
d
) holds by applying the following argument:

\displaystyle|{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V;q+K+1,n)|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{j\in V}\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)\mu(\sigma_{i})+f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)\mu(v^{j}_{i})

\displaystyle-2f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma_{i}}^{\downarrow,\sigma}\cap{\sigma_{i}}^{\downarrow,v^{j}}\right|\right)\mu(\sigma_{i})\bigg|

\displaystyle\overset{(d1)}{\leq}\sum_{j\in V}\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)\mu({\sigma_{i}})+f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)\mu(v^{j}_{i})

\displaystyle+2f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma_{i}}^{\downarrow,\sigma}\cap{\sigma_{i}}^{\downarrow,v^{j}}\right|\right)\mu(\sigma_{i})

\displaystyle\overset{(d2)}{\leq}4mU\sum_{i=q+K+1}^{n}f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i),

where (
d1
) holds by applying twice the triangular inequality, and (
d2
) holds by observing that
f_{\bm{\beta}}\left(\left|{\sigma_{i}}^{\downarrow,\sigma}\cap{\sigma_{i}}^{\downarrow,v^{j}}\right|\right)\leq f_{\bm{\beta}}(n-i)
and
\mu_{i}\leq U
.
We have established

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V)}{{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{12U\sum_{j=2}^{n-(q+K)}\beta_{j}\binom{n-(q+K)}{j}}{u\sum_{j=0}^{n-(q+2)}\beta_{j+2}\binom{n-(q+2)}{j}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{12U}{u}\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{t-K}\beta_{j}\binom{t-K}{j}}{\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}\beta_{j+2}\binom{t-2}{j}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\epsilon,

for
K\geq g\left(\frac{12U}{u\epsilon}\right)
. Therefore, by choosing
K=g\left(\frac{12U}{u\epsilon}\right)
, we have

{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\bar{\sigma},V)\leq(1+\epsilon){\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\sigma^{*},V).

Finally, on the one hand, observe that the while loop and the computation of
\{f_{\bm{\beta}}(0),\ldots,f_{{\bm{\beta}}}(n-1)\}
can be done in polynomial time. On the other hand, we can minimize
{\mathrm{d}^{\mu}_{\bm{\beta}}}(\cdot,V;q+1,q+K+1)
by searching over all possible cases; when there are no majority alternatives, there are
O\left(K!\binom{n}{K}\right)=O\left(n^{K}\right)
cases to be checked. Evaluating each case can be done in time
O(mKn)
, and so the overall running time of the algorithm turns out to be
O(n^{K+1}mK)
.
∎

G.
Computations

Suppose
\beta_{j}=(\alpha-1)^{j}
for
\alpha>1
and
j\in[2,n]
. Pick
K\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle h(t):=\frac{\sum_{j=2}^{t-K}(\alpha-1)^{j}\binom{t-K}{j}}{\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}(\alpha-1)^{j+2}\binom{t-2}{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{t-K}-(\alpha-1)(t-K)-1}{(\alpha-1)^{2}\alpha^{t-2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{-K}-\alpha^{-t}(\alpha-1)(t-K)-\alpha^{-t}}{(\alpha-1)^{2}\alpha^{-2}}

Now,
\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}h(t)=\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}\frac{\alpha^{-K}-\alpha^{-t}(\alpha-1)(t-K)-\alpha^{-t}}{(\alpha-1)^{2}\alpha^{-2}}=\frac{\alpha^{-K}}{\left(\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha}\right)^{2}}
, as
-\alpha^{-t}(\alpha-1)(t-K)-\alpha^{-t}\leq 0
. Therefore,
\frac{\alpha^{-K}}{\left(\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha}\right)^{2}}\leq\epsilon
if and only if
K\geq\log_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{(\alpha-1)^{2}\epsilon}\right)
.

Suppose
\beta_{j}=j+1
and
j\in[2,n]
. Pick
K\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle h(t):=\frac{\sum_{j=2}^{t-K}(j+1)\binom{t-K}{j}}{\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}(j+3)\binom{t-2}{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2^{t-K-1}-2)(t-K)+2^{t-K}-1}{2^{t-3}(t+4)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(t-K)(2^{-K-1}-2\times 2^{-t})+2^{-K}-2^{-t}}{2^{-3}(t+4)}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{(t-K)2^{-K-1}+2^{-K}}{2^{-3}(t+4)}\overset{(b)}{\leq}4\times 2^{-K},

where (
a
) holds because
t\geq K
, and (
b
) holds because
K\geq 1
. In particular, note that
\lim_{t\to\infty}h(t)=4\times 2^{-K}
. Therefore,
\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}h(t)=4\times 2^{-K}
, which, in turn, implies that
\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}h(t)\leq\epsilon
if and only if
K\geq\log_{2}(4/\epsilon)
.

Finally, suppose
\beta_{j}=1+(-1)^{j}
for all
j\in[2,n]
. Pick
K\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle h(t):=\frac{\sum_{j=2}^{t-K}(1+(-1)^{j})\binom{t-K}{j}}{\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}(1+(-1)^{j+2})\binom{t-2}{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{j=2}^{t-K}(1+(-1)^{j})\binom{t-K}{j}}{\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}(1+(-1)^{j})\binom{t-2}{j}}=\frac{\sum_{j=2}^{t-K}\binom{t-K}{j}+\sum_{j=2}^{t-K}(-1)^{j}\binom{t-K}{j}}{\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}\binom{t-2}{j}+\sum_{j=0}^{t-2}(-1)^{j}\binom{t-2}{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2^{t-K}-(t-K)-1)+\delta_{t-K}-(1-(t-K))}{2^{t-2}+\delta_{t-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{t-K}}{2^{t-2}}=4\times 2^{-K}.

In particular, note that
\lim_{t\to\infty}h(t)=4\times 2^{-K}
. Therefore,
\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}h(t)=4\times 2^{-K}
, which, in turn, implies that
\sup_{t\geq K\vee 2}h(t)\leq\epsilon
if and only if
K\geq\log_{2}(4/\epsilon)
.
